The war ends on 30 April 1975 with the fall of Saigon and the unconditional surrender of the Republic of Vietnam, on the historical timeline: rung (a), collapse. The Paris Agreement had removed the American forces and kept the PAVN in the South; Congress had written the end of American combat and the limits on the President into law; American military aid had fallen; and the government in Saigon, more stable than at any time since 1963 but narrowly based, economically weakened and dependent on American supply, faced an enemy that had rebuilt after 1972 and chose its moment. The decisive blows were Hanoi's, at Phuoc Long and Ban Me Thuot; the decisive errors were Saigon's, in the order to abandon the Highlands and the contradictory orders to I Corps.

Hanoi's posture never changed. It was committed to armed reunification throughout, from the Ninth Plenum of December 1963 to the Ho Chi Minh Campaign, through the losses of 1965–68 and 1972, and it accepted the Paris Agreement as a step towards that end. The US war in Vietnam, from the advisory years to 1973, cost more than 58,000 American dead. The Vietnamese dead, military and civilian, northern and southern, are counted in the millions by most estimates, and the record of them is less precise than the record of the Americans. That is the war as it happened, and the war the exercise begins from.

The squad guns and the marksmen's rifles of the ARVN, and about 9 million rounds of linked 5.56mm from the divisional hoards of I and II Corps, are in the PAVN's hands by the end of April 1975, with the carbines of a million men¹⁷⁴.

The Republic's defence of every besieged town had been built since 1971 around American bombers and American coordinates, and both were gone by the end of 1973¹⁷⁶.

The officers the reforms made fought the last campaign as well as any part of the RVNAF fought it, and their best staff plan was taken by the President and executed without its method. About 12,000 of the board's officers were captured in 1975 and sent to the re-education camps with the rest of the officer corps; the board's files, with the evaluation report of every officer since 1964, were in the Joint General Staff's compound on 30 April⁶³.

### Mechanism

The reforms change the last three years by making the Republic's officers more exact about what they lacked, and in one decision that precision is used against them. In 1974 the battalion and regimental commanders at Thuong Duc, Duc Duc and Phu Loc fought under a ration that silenced the fires their doctrine assumed, and wrote the best account anywhere of what it cost; the losses stand at history's. In November 1974 a seminar of the Dalat staff college wrote a plan for the defence of the Republic on reduced resources that a President could have used. On 14 March 1975 Thieu took its conclusion and none of its method, and the retreat from the Highlands went as it went historically. At Xuan Loc and in three other places, well-led divisions and brigades fought as the 18th Division did historically, and none of it changed the end.

I leave Phase VI's end vector at the historical state on every headline variable. The phase ends after the Republic's fall, and the gain carried in from Phase V, a band in ARVN effectiveness in the regiments and battalions, is answered here by the ration, which made a better-led battalion of 1974 bleed as history's did, and by the decisions of March 1975, which were taken by the President and the corps commanders and executed in days.

### Enemy response and initiative

The PAVN's plan for 1975 was Hanoi's own, taken in the 21st Plenum's line and tested at Phuoc Long, and the officer reforms played no part in it. Its divisions met better-led ARVN units at Xuan Loc and on the coast and did what the PAVN had always done against a better unit: bypassed it, isolated it and let the collapse around it decide the matter. At Ban Me Thuot it struck where the corps commander did not expect it, which is a corps commander's failure and not a battalion's.

### Causal chains

**C007-5** [war-shaping] (begins in Phase I, EV007-1) The classes of 1957 onward are educated on a 2026 American doctrine in which the infantry finds the enemy and the fires destroy him, and from 1965 they command the battalions → at An Loc and Kontum in 1972 their staffs use American air with a precision history's did not, and the army's plans for every besieged town rest on it → when the ration of 1974 silences the fires, the same officers are the only ones in the Republic who can state exactly what the army can no longer do, and a seminar under Colonel Trieu at Dalat writes in November 1974 a plan for a three-month withdrawal to a defensible Republic → Thieu takes its conclusion and none of its method on 14 March 1975 and orders the Highlands abandoned in three days by Route 7, so that the most rigorous staff work the reforms produced became the warrant for the decision that lost the war fastest.

### Downstream

There is no later phase. About 12,000 of the board's officers went to the camps in 1975, and the board's files, with an evaluation report on every officer since 1964, were in the Joint General Staff's compound on 30 April; I do not claim their capture as a consequence the record shows, beyond noting that the files existed and were not destroyed.

### Clock

The clock had run out before this phase began. Case–Church, the War Powers Resolution and the aid cuts of 1974 ran on their historical dates, and no improvement in an ally's officer corps reached a Congress that had decided to stop paying for the war. The one American contact with the reforms in these years was the Defense Attaché Office's reports on the ARVN's officers, which were more favourable than history's and changed no vote.

## §4.H Historical comparison

Against history, the cumulative force now has an ARVN whose battalions were led as the historical army's never were. On the historical course 80 of 133 battalion commanders had held command a year or less in May 1971, and 46 of 133 battalions were led by captains¹⁵³; in this war the captain-led battalion was about one in ten by the end of 1966, battalion commands ran eighteen months from 1965, about four battalions in five were commanded by an officer a board had chosen by the end of 1967, and about 30 commanders were relieved for cause in the first year of reviews⁶³. I judge that the ARVN lost about a third fewer battalions rendered ineffective in 1966–67 than historically, a judgment on a rate shown so far on Route 13 and in the target notes taken in 1967, and not a measurement.

The officer corps changed its character from below. About 5,500 sergeants were commissioned by the end of 1968, the diploma bar to major was gone from 1964, and the mobilization's platoon leaders came from a 32-week course and the ranks rather than a 24-week crash course⁶³. The price was a sergeant shortage in the new territorials of 1968–69.

In the battles, the margins are small and real: the 57th Regiment reaching the Cua Viet as a regiment, perhaps 500 fewer dead on Highway 1 in May 1972, a third more of the 42nd Regiment out of Tan Canh, perhaps 200 fewer ARVN killed in the first phase of Tet. On the American side, the educated company commanders at Albany saved perhaps ten to twenty lives, and across 1965–69 I would put the saving at a rate of one to three percent of American deaths in small-unit fighting, a judgment shown so far in one event that does not move the casualty band⁶³.

At the top nothing changed that the lever did not change. One or two division commands in ten were filled from the list against the palace's preference by 1967; Truong reached the 1st Division by the list and I Corps by default; the list became advisory in 1970; and the corps commanders who lost Quang Tri in 1972 and the Highlands in 1975 were chosen as they were chosen historically. The end is unchanged: Saigon fell on 30 April 1975.

## §4.N Net assessment and end state

The end state stays at rung (a), collapse on the historical timeline. This is the most substantial provision the ARVN has received in this series, and it does not move the rung, for a reason the memorandum stated before the war was run: education and selection stop at the level where the war was decided. Rung (b) would require the Republic to fail only for the withdrawal of American combat forces and not on the historical schedule; it still falls on that schedule because the decisive failures of 1972 and 1975 were at corps and in the palace, because the American lever that protected the general-officer list lapsed in 1970, and because the officer corps the reforms built was educated on a doctrine of American fire that the agreement of 1973 and the aid cuts of 1974 took away. Rung (c), the RVN defeating a conventional offensive with American air, was already shown in 1972 and is not new here.

What the reforms have changed is the distance between the Republic's battalions and its government. By 1967 the ARVN's battalions are at the cap of this exercise's bands and its corps are where they were; the war's binding deficits are political and at the top, as the Theater Needs Assessment said they would be. A provision that chose corps commanders as the board chose battalion commanders, and kept the choice after the Americans left, would be the next test of that judgment.

The war's shape has not outgrown its phases, and I propose no redraw. Chapters P1 and P2 are over the length at which the checker flags a chapter, about 18,800 and 20,000 words, and I flag both for consolidation at the next checkpoint.

## §5 Delta ledger

**Forces gained.** P007a: the 2026 US officer education continuum for every Army and Marine officer commissioned from 1957, with the 1957–63 classes already through it in 1964; about 1,500–2,500 instructor and staff spaces in the United States and about 4–6 percent of company- and field-grade officers in school at any time. P007b: the same continuum in Vietnamese for every ARVN officer commissioned from 1957, with seven classes of about 10,000–14,000 serving officers already through the branch course in 1964, and about 350–500 Americans as faculty advisers. P007c: the Officer Selection and Promotion Board, the evaluation report, central command lists, relief for cause, field commissions and the ranked general-officer list under the aid condition, with about 60–90 Americans¹⁵³.

**Forces lost or changed in status.** The general-officer list made advisory on 12 January 1970; the board's American cell withdrawn in March 1973 with the advisers; the board's files left in the Joint General Staff's compound on 30 April 1975⁶³.

**Variables moved at phase ends.**
- ARVN.effectiveness: +2 → +3 at the end of P2 and P3; +1 → +2 at the end of P4; 0 → +1 at the end of P5; P6 unchanged, at the historical state after the fall.
- No other headline band moved. GVN.political_stability is held at its bands: the coups of 1964–65 were already at the historical maximum and the board added a grievance rather than a coup, and the palace's purge of 1970–71 replaced a petition rather than a plot. US.casualties is held: the saving is a few percent of small-unit deaths, below a band. ENEMY.engagement_control is held: the enemy still chose every fight, and chose more of them against the unreformed units.

**New categories.** A central command list for ARVN battalions and regiments, from 1964; relief for cause on a thirty-day review, from 1965; commission from the ranks without the diploma bar, from 1966; a ranked list for division command under an aid condition, 1965–69.

## §6 Changed / not changed

### What has changed

- The ARVN's battalions were commanded from 1965–67 by officers chosen on two raters' reports for eighteen-month commands and relieved when they failed; the captain-led battalion and the six-month command, both features of the historical army as late as 1971, were gone by 1967. This is the largest single gain to ARVN fighting power in this series and it sits at the level where most of the war's contacts were fought.
- Better battalions were attacked less: the 272nd's ambush on Route 13 was declined on 4 May 1966, and I judge about a third fewer battalions were rendered ineffective in 1966–67, a gain that shows in no metric because the enemy chose not to make the fights that would have shown it.
- The companies of 1964–65, and the American companies of 1965–69, were led by officers educated to scout, plan fires and write orders; at Ba Gia, Albany and Tan Canh more men came out than history's.
- The officer corps began to change its class from below: about 5,500 sergeants were commissioned by the end of 1968, and the mobilization needed no crash course for its platoon leaders.
- One division in the worst state in the army, the 25th, was given a commander from the list in 1967 and improved its desertion and its contact rate within the year; Truong reached the 1st Division by the list in 1966.
- In 1972 regiments held where the historical ones dissolved, and the road south of Quang Tri cost perhaps 500 fewer lives.

### What has not changed

- The corps and the palace: the general-officer list was only as strong as the aid condition, became advisory in 1970, and never governed the corps; Quang Tri in 1972 and the Highlands in 1975 were lost by orders from generals no board had chosen.
- The enemy still chose every battle; he declined the better battalion and attacked the one beside it, and he read the board's lists through an agent in its own secretariat.
- An educated officer corps under a palace-chosen top became a faction in the palace's eyes, and the President's answer in 1970–71 removed forty of its best commanders before Lam Son 719.
- The doctrine the officers learned deepened the army's dependence on American fire; in 1974 they could state exactly what the ration had taken and could not replace it.
- My Lai happened, and the late-war decay of American discipline ran on its historical course; the lieutenant's education did not reach the command climate above him.
- The American clock, the withdrawal and the aid cuts ran on their historical dates, and Saigon fell on 30 April 1975.

### Trajectory Log entry

Memorandum 007 educated every American and ARVN officer commissioned from 1957 to the 2026 standard and gave the ARVN a promotion board that chose its battalion commanders on merit, kept them eighteen months and relieved them when they failed. From 1966 the ARVN's battalions were led as the historical army's never were, and the enemy answered as he answers every better unit: he declined to fight them. The 272nd Regiment withdrew from its ambush on Route 13 rather than meet a battalion that walked its flanks, and attacked a Regional Force convoy instead; about a third fewer battalions were broken in 1966–67, and no metric showed it. But the reform stopped where the American lever stopped. The list for generals became advisory in 1970, the palace purged the colonels who asked for it back, and Quang Tri in 1972 and the Highlands in 1975 were lost by generals no board had chosen. The army's best staff plan became Thieu's warrant for the retreat of March 1975. Saigon falls on 30 April 1975, at rung (a).

### Positions recorded

- **POS007-1** · vars: ARVN.leadership, ARVN.effectiveness, ENEMY.engagement_control · In this war the ARVN's battalions from 1965–67 are commanded by officers a board chose on written criteria and two raters' reports, for eighteen months, with relief for cause; this puts ARVN effectiveness at the cap of the bands in 1966–68, and the enemy answers by declining the better battalion and attacking the unreformed unit beside it, so the gain appears as battalions not lost rather than in any metric (007 §4.P2; EV007-2, EV007-3)
- **POS007-2** · vars: ARVN.leadership, ADVISE.leverage, GVN.political_stability · The ranked list for generals and division commands works only while the American aid condition protects it; in this war it filled one or two division commands in ten against the palace's preference by 1967, became advisory on 12 January 1970, and never governed the corps, so that the decisive commands of 1972 and 1975 were chosen as they were historically (007 §4.P4, §4.P5; EV007-4, EV007-5)
- **POS007-3** · vars: GVN.political_stability, ARVN.leadership, INTEL.penetration · An educated field-grade officer corps whose promotion is blocked at the top becomes a faction in the palace's eyes; in this war the colonels' petition of September 1970 was answered by moving about forty regimental and battalion commanders out of the field before Lam Son 719, and COSVN targeted the passed-over officers from 1968 with small yield (007 §4.P3, §4.P4; EV007-5, EV000-52)
- **POS007-4** · vars: US.unit_quality, US.casualties, POP.civilian_harm · American officer education from 1957 improves platoon and company leadership and saves American lives at a rate of perhaps one to three percent of small-unit deaths in 1965–69, below a band; it does not prevent My Lai or the late-war decay of discipline, which came from the command climate above the lieutenant (007 §4.P2, §4.P3; EV000-23)

### Persons recorded

- **Phan Khac Minh** · Brigadier General; chairman of the Officer Selection and Promotion Board from 1 January 1964, formerly head of the General Staff's personnel directorate 1955–57 · invented in 007 · Sent to a training camp by Diem for refusing to promote the Can Lao's men; struck the bargain with Khanh in February 1964 that confirmed eleven of twenty-three coup promotions and refused twelve
- **Robert E. Lindqvist** · Colonel, US Army; chief of the American advisory cell to the Officer Selection Board from 1964 · invented in 007 · Built the evaluation system's trainers' course at Fort Benning; estimated in 1966 that half the sale of commands had ended and half had changed its name
- **Bui Quang Trieu** · ARVN officer commissioned from Thu Duc in 1957; major commanding 2nd Battalion, 9th Regiment, 5th Division from 1965; colonel commanding a 5th Division regiment from 1968 and the 3rd Division's 57th Regiment from 1971; Dalat faculty from 1972 · invented in 007 · Walked his flanks on Route 13 on 4 May 1966 and the 272nd declined its ambush; refused to sign the colonels' petition; held the Thach Han bridges at Quang Tri on 1 May 1972; led the Dalat seminar that wrote the reduced-resources study of November 1974
- **Dang Huu Thoi** · Lieutenant Colonel, commander of the VC 272nd Regiment in 1966 · invented in 007 · Withdrew the regiment from its ambush in the Xa Cam rubber on 4 May 1966 without firing, and ambushed an RF convoy at Bau Bang on 21 May
- **Vu Ngoc Hoan** · Colonel, commander of the ARVN 25th Division from March 1967 · invented in 007 · The list's second name, chosen by Thieu under the aid condition against Ky's candidate; put the division's battalions into the Hau Nghia districts at night and cut its desertion by about a quarter
- **Truong Cong Hy** · Lieutenant, clerk of the Officer Selection Board's secretariat 1965–67 · invented in 007 · Copied every command list for the B2 Front's military intelligence; arrested on 11 October 1967
- **Nguyen Dac Khiet** · Colonel, commanding a regiment of the 9th Division in 1970 · invented in 007 · Drafted the colonels' petition of September 1970 for the restoration of the general-officer list; moved to the Dalat faculty in the purge of December 1970
